\section{Question and answer}\label{sec:answer}

\paragraph{Question.}
\begin{enumerate}
\item Fourier transform the three-$\rho$ part of Group I, Row II (your expression, with the full bracket of logarithms) to coordinate space.
\item Take all three-$\rho$ terms proportional to $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ and show that they cancel. You suspected that the earlier input did not contain all of them.
\item Take all two-$\rho$ terms proportional to $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ and show that they reproduce the JIMWLK evolution of the target and the BFKL evolution of the projectile.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Answer.}
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{The Fourier transform} (Part~\ref{part:ft}, every step written out).
\begin{itemize}
\item The bracket in your message lacks the term $-\bk^i\log(\vee/k^+)$, which \texttt{Group\_I.tex} and \texttt{main.tex} have. The $\xi$-integral form of \texttt{main.tex} confirms the term (Table~\ref{tab:xi}). It contains no $\Lambda$.
\item With the full bracket, $\mathfrak B^i=A^i\log(k^+/\Lambda)+R^i$, with $A^i=-E^i-2\bk^i-D^i$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:Bsplit}). So the coefficients of $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ and $\log(\vee/k^+)$ are $A^i$ and $-A^i$. $R^i$ has no large logarithm.
\item Only the part of the kernel that is odd under $x\leftrightarrow y$ contributes. The transform is
\begin{gather*}
\int d^2\bk\,d^2\bp\,e^{i\bk\cdot(w-y)}e^{i\bp\cdot(y-x)}\frac{A^i}{\bk^2\bp^2}\bigg|_{\rm odd}=2\pi i\,\big[\mathfrak F_\beta^i+\mathfrak F_\alpha^i\big],\\
\mathfrak F^i_\beta=\KK^i(x-w)\!\int_zK(w,y,z)-\KK^i(y-w)\!\int_zK(w,x,z),\qquad
\mathfrak F^i_\alpha=-\big[\KK^i(x-w)-\KK^i(y-w)\big]\!\int_zK(x,y,z).
\end{gather*}
\item The row in coordinate space is Eq.~\eqref{eq:RowIIcoord}. Its prefactor is $-\frac i2\NN$ for $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ and $+\frac i2\NP$ for $\log(\vee/k^+)$.
\end{itemize}
\item \textbf{The earlier input was complete for $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$.} \texttt{Evolution\_three\_rho.tex} has exactly $A^i\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ for this row, and the transform above is the one used before. The new pieces of the full bracket multiply $\log(\vee/k^+)$ or no large logarithm. So the three-$\rho$ result does not change.
\item \textbf{The three-$\rho$ terms proportional to $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$} (Part~\ref{part:three}).
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{As written they do not all cancel.} Of 48 colour networks, 28 cancel and 20 do not. Every left-over comes from Group I:
\begin{itemize}
\item the $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ part of Row II in the $U(w)\mathbb A^{(3)}$ term;
\item part 2 of Row III (the edge kernel $\Phi$), together with a missing term $\mathcal D$.
\end{itemize}
\item \emph{With fixes F3 and F4 all 40 remaining networks cancel exactly}, and a pointwise test gives $10^{-16}$.
\item \emph{F4 is now understood} (Sec.~\ref{sec:alpha}). The $-2\bk^i$ term of the bracket is exactly the commutator $[\mathbb N^{(2)},\mathbb A^{(1)}]$ of the normalization with the LO amplitude. \texttt{main.tex} sets this commutator to zero, but it is not zero. Ordering the virtual factor in $p^+$ (soft part to the left of $\mathbb A^{(1)}$, hard part to the right) is exactly F4. With it the gluon exchanged between the two charges, $\mathfrak F_\alpha$, comes with $\log(\vee/k^+)$ (projectile) instead of $\log(k^+/\Lambda)$ (target).
\end{itemize}
\item \textbf{The two-$\rho$ terms proportional to $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ give JIMWLK$\otimes$LO exactly} (Part~\ref{part:jimwlk}). This holds after fixes F1--F6, in all 13 kernel structures, pointwise in $z$ and with exact coefficients. This is the evolution of the target: the coefficient of $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ is $-\partial/\partial\log\Lambda$, which comes from gluons softer than the measured one.
\item \textbf{BFKL$\otimes$LO is not the coefficient of $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$.} It is a different function (Table~\ref{T:tab:appF}). The projectile evolves with $\log\vee$, i.e.\ with the coefficient of $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ \emph{plus} that of $\log(\vee/k^+)$. With fixes P1, F3--F6 (Part~\ref{part:bfkl}):
\begin{itemize}
\item the symmetrized three-$\rho$ terms of $\log\vee$ cancel (4 kernel structures, 44 networks);
\item the two-$\rho$ terms of $\log\vee$ equal BFKL$\otimes$LO exactly (9 kernel structures, exact coefficients).
\end{itemize}
\item \textbf{Together} (Part~\ref{part:sum}):
\begin{equation*}
\boxed{\;\frac{d^3N}{d^3k}\bigg|_{\rm LL}=\underbrace{\log\frac{k^+}{\Lambda}}_{\delta Y_T}\,\text{JIMWLK}\otimes\text{LO}+\underbrace{\log\frac{\vee}{k^+}}_{\delta Y_P}\,\text{BFKL}\otimes\text{LO},\qquad \delta Y_T+\delta Y_P=\log\frac\vee\Lambda=\delta Y .\;}
\end{equation*}
The target evolves over the rapidity interval below the measured gluon and the projectile over the interval above it. In terms of your two logarithms, $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ multiplies JIMWLK$\otimes$LO, and $\log(\vee/k^+)$ multiplies BFKL$\otimes$LO$-$JIMWLK$\otimes$LO.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}l >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.62\textwidth} c c@{}}
fix & what & $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ & $\log\vee$\\ \hline
F1 & \texttt{4rho.tex}, first part: drop the $-8N_c\,\KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(x'-w)\,K(y,x',z)$ term & 2$\rho$ & 2$\rho$\\
F2 & \texttt{4rho.tex}, $dN^{(1)}$ term 2: repeated index; correct term $3f^{abc}f^{deg}\rho^{b'}(x')\rho^c(y)U^{ag}(y)U^{db'}(w')U^{eb}(z)$ & 2$\rho$ & 2$\rho$\\
F3 & Group I Row III: part 2 ($\Phi$ terms) $\to\mathcal D$ & 3$\rho$, 2$\rho$ & --\\
F4 & Group I Row II, $U(w)\mathbb A^{(3)}$: $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ goes with $-\log(\vee/k^+)$, not $\log(k^+/\Lambda)$ ($p^+$ ordering, Sec.~\ref{sec:alpha}) & 3$\rho$, 2$\rho$ & 3$\rho$, 2$\rho$\\

\label{tab:fixes}
\end{table}
Everything uses your convention $U^{\dagger bc}=U^{bc}=U^{cb}$.

\paragraph{How this note is organized.} Part~\ref{part:ft} is new. Parts~\ref{part:three}--\ref{part:bfkl} and the appendices collect the earlier notes \texttt{ThreeRho\_cancellation}, \texttt{TwoRho\_evolution}, \texttt{LogVeeKplus\_BFKL} and \texttt{ThreeRho\_to\_TwoRho}, with cross-references updated. Part~\ref{part:bfkl} now compares the coefficient of $\log\vee$ directly with BFKL$\otimes$LO; these tables are new. Equation and table labels of the form \lab{C:}, \lab{T:}, \lab{B:}, \lab{R:} in the source refer to those notes.

%==========================================================================================
\section{Conventions}\label{sec:conv}
\paragraph{Units.}
\begin{equation}
\NN\equiv\frac{1}{(2\pi)^3}\,\frac{g^4}{16\pi^5}\,\frac{1}{k^+}\,\log\frac{\vee}{\Lambda},\qquad
\NP\equiv\frac{1}{(2\pi)^3}\,\frac{g^4}{16\pi^5}\,\frac{1}{k^+}\,\log\frac{\vee}{k^+}.
\label{eq:NN}
\end{equation}
So $\frac{1}{(2\pi)^3}\frac{ig^4}{16\pi^5}\frac{1}{k^+}\log\frac\vee\Lambda=i\NN$, $\frac{1}{(2\pi)^3}\frac{ig^4}{8\pi^5}\frac{1}{k^+}\log\frac\vee\Lambda=2i\NN$, $\frac{1}{(2\pi)^3}\frac{ig^4}{32\pi^5}\frac{1}{k^+}\log\frac\vee\Lambda=\frac i2\NN$, and the same with $\NP$ for $\log(\vee/k^+)$. A coefficient written as a number (e.g.\ $2i$) is in units of $\NN$ or $\NP$.

\paragraph{Logarithms.} Your rules are $\log(\Lambda/k^+)=-\log(\vee/\Lambda)+\log(\vee/k^+)$ and $\log((\vee-k^+)/\Lambda)=\log(\vee/\Lambda)+\log(1-k^+/\vee)$. We write $L_\Lambda=\log(\vee/\Lambda)$, $L_k=\log(\vee/k^+)$, and
\begin{equation}
\delta Y_T\equiv\log\frac{k^+}{\Lambda}=L_\Lambda-L_k,\qquad \delta Y_P\equiv\log\frac{\vee}{k^+}=L_k,\qquad \delta Y_T+\delta Y_P=\log\frac{\vee}{\Lambda}.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Kernels.} $\KK(X)\equiv X/X^2$ (a two-vector), $\KK(-X)=-\KK(X)$, $\KK(X)\cdot\KK(X)=1/X^2$, and
\begin{equation}
K(a,b,z)\equiv\KK(a-z)\cdot\KK(b-z)=\frac{(a-z)\cdot(b-z)}{(a-z)^2(b-z)^2},\qquad K(a,a,z)=\frac{1}{(a-z)^2}.
\end{equation}
All transverse positions except the measured-gluon positions $w$ (amplitude) and $w'$ (conjugate amplitude) are integrated, and the phase $e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}$ is always present. The unobserved (soft or hard) gluon is at $z$.

\paragraph{Charges.} The valence charges obey
\begin{equation}
\big[\rho^a(x),\rho^b(y)\big]=if^{abc}\rho^c(x)\,\delta^{(2)}(x-y).
\label{eq:comm}
\end{equation}
Every ordered product of three charges is written as its fully symmetrized part $S(ABC)=\frac16(ABC+ACB+BAC+BCA+CAB+CBA)$ plus two-$\rho$ terms (App.~\ref{R:sec:identity}, Eqs.~\eqref{R:eq:plain}--\eqref{R:eq:antiA}):
\begin{equation}
ABC\to S(ABC),\qquad A\{B,C\}\to2S(ABC),\qquad\{B,C\}A\to2S(ABC)\qquad\text{(three-}\rho\text{ part)}.
\end{equation}
$S(ABC)$ behaves as a product of commuting charges. After reordering, the two charges of a two-$\rho$ term form a symmetric product and also behave as commuting variables.

\paragraph{Complex conjugate.} A three-$\rho$ term has an imaginary coefficient, and $S(\rho\rho\rho)$ is Hermitian. Its c.c.\ is therefore
\begin{equation}
\text{c.c.\ of a three-}\rho\text{ term}=-\big(\text{same term with }P\leftrightarrow P'\big),
\label{eq:cc}
\end{equation}
where $P$, $P'$ are the measured-gluon positions. A two-$\rho$ term has a real coefficient, so its c.c.\ is the same term with $P\leftrightarrow P'$ (no minus sign).

\paragraph{Colour.} $(T^a)_{bc}=-if^{abc}$, (F) $f^{acd}f^{bcd}=N_c\delta^{ab}$, and for adjoint Wilson lines
\begin{align}
&\text{(U1)}\quad U^{ab}(x)\,U^{ac}(x)=\delta^{bc},\label{eq:U1}\\
&\text{(U2)}\quad f^{abc}\,U^{ad}(x)\,U^{be}(x)=f^{deh}\,U^{ch}(x).\label{eq:U2}
\end{align}
Your notes use
\begin{equation}
U^{\dagger\,bc}=U^{bc}=U^{cb},
\label{eq:Usym}
\end{equation}
and we follow this. Steps that need it are marked (U1$^\star$), (U2$^\star$). They are exact only for a symmetric $U$.

%==========================================================================================
\part{The Fourier transform of Group I, Row II}\label{part:ft}
%==========================================================================================
\section{The term and its three pieces}\label{sec:ft-input}

\paragraph{The term.} The three-$\rho$ part of Group I, Row II, as in your message:
\begin{align}
\frac{d^3N_{2\,\rm NLO}}{d^3k}={}&-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^3}\,\frac{g^4f^{abc}}{64\pi^6}\,\frac{1}{k^+}\int_{w,w'}e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}\int d^2\bk\,d^2\bp\int_{x,y}e^{i\bk\cdot(w-y)}\,e^{i\bp\cdot(y-x)}\,\frac{(x'-w')^i}{(x'-w')^2\,\bp^2\bk^2}\;\mathfrak B^i(\bk,\bp)\notag\\
&\times\Big[U^{ab'}(x')\rho^{b'}(x')-U^{ab'}(w')\rho^{b'}(x')\Big]\Big[U^{ab}(w)\{\rho^b(x),\rho^c(y)\}-U^{bd}(x)U^{ce}(y)\{\rho^d(x),\rho^e(y)\}\Big]+{\rm c.c.}
\label{eq:ft-term}
\end{align}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Notation.} Inside the transverse integral $\bk$ and $\bp$ (bold) are integration momenta. The plain $k$ in $e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}$ and $k^+$ belong to the measured gluon. We also write $\bq\equiv\bk-\bp$, and
\begin{equation}
L_\Lambda\equiv\log\frac{\vee}{\Lambda},\qquad L_k\equiv\log\frac{\vee}{k^+},\qquad L_T\equiv\log\frac{k^+}{\Lambda}=L_\Lambda-L_k .
\end{equation}
\item \emph{c.c.} Group I always comes with its complex conjugate (as in \texttt{Evolution\_three\_rho.tex}); we write it explicitly.
\item \emph{Colour.} As written, the indices $a$ and $b$ each appear three times in the first term of the second bracket. That term is $U(w)\mathbb A^{(3)}$ with $\mathbb A^{(3)h}\propto f^{hbc}\{\rho^b(x),\rho^c(y)\}$, so we read it as $U^{ah}(w)f^{hbc}\{\rho^b(x),\rho^c(y)\}$. The second term, $f^{abc}U^{bd}(x)U^{ce}(y)\{\rho^d(x),\rho^e(y)\}$, is $\bar{\mathbb A}^{(3)a}$ (rotated charges) and is unchanged.
\item $(x'-w')^i/(x'-w')^2=\KK^i(x'-w')$ is the LO emission kernel of the conjugate amplitude.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{The bracket.} With your rules $\log(\Lambda/k^+)=-L_\Lambda+L_k$ and $\log((\vee-k^+)/\Lambda)=L_\Lambda+\log(1-k^+/\vee)$, the bracket of your message is
\begin{align}
\mathfrak B^i_{\rm msg}={}&\big[-L_\Lambda+L_k\big]E^i-2\bk^iL_\Lambda-2\bk^i\,\ell_1+C^i\,\ell_2-D^i\big[L_\Lambda+\ell_3\big],
\label{eq:Bmsg}
\end{align}
with the three momentum functions
\begin{equation}
E^i\equiv\frac{\bk^2\bp^i-(\bk\cdot\bp)\,\bk^i}{\bq^2},\qquad
C^i\equiv\frac{(2\bp^2-2\bk\cdot\bp+\bk^2)\,\bk^i+\bk^2\bp^i}{\bq^2},\qquad
D^i\equiv\frac{\bp^2\bk^i+\bk^2\bp^i}{\bq^2},
\label{eq:ECD}
\end{equation}
and the three logarithms
\begin{equation}
\ell_1\equiv\log\frac{k^+\bp^2}{\vee\bk^2+k^+\bp^2},\qquad
\ell_2\equiv\log\Big[1+\Big(\frac{\vee}{k^+}-1\Big)\frac{\bk^2}{\bq^2}\Big],\qquad
\ell_3\equiv\log\Big(1-\frac{k^+}{\vee}\Big).
\end{equation}
\paragraph{A useful identity.} Since $2\bp^2-2\bk\cdot\bp+\bk^2=(\bk-\bp)^2+\bp^2=\bq^2+\bp^2$,
\begin{equation}
C^i=\frac{\bq^2\bk^i+\bp^2\bk^i+\bk^2\bp^i}{\bq^2}=\bk^i+D^i .
\label{eq:CD}
\end{equation}

\subsection{A term that is missing in the message}\label{sec:ft-missing}
In \texttt{Group\_I.tex} and in the coordinate-space $\mathbb A^{(3)}_2$ of \texttt{main.tex}, the same bracket ends with one more term, $-\bk^i\log(\vee/k^+)$:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\;\mathfrak B^i=\mathfrak B^i_{\rm msg}-\bk^i\,L_k\;}
\label{eq:Bfull}
\end{equation}
\texttt{main.tex} also gives this bracket in momentum space \emph{before} the longitudinal integral is done ($\xi=p^+/k^+$):
\begin{align}
\mathfrak B^i_\xi={}&\log\frac{\Lambda}{k^+}\,E^i-2\bk^iL_\Lambda-2\bk^i\ell_1\notag\\
&-\int_{\Lambda/k^+}^{1-\Lambda/k^+}d\xi\,\frac{\xi}{\big(\xi\bq^2+(1-\xi)\bk^2\big)(1-\xi)}\Big[\Big(\frac2\xi(\bp^2-\bk\cdot\bp)-\bp^2+2\bk\cdot\bp\Big)\bk^i+\frac1\xi\bk^2\bp^i\Big].
\label{eq:Bxi}
\end{align}
Table~\ref{tab:xi} compares the three forms at random momenta (component $i=1$, $k^+=1$, $\Lambda=10^{-4}$). The $\xi$ form agrees with $\mathfrak B$ up to terms of order $k^+/\vee$, and differs from $\mathfrak B_{\rm msg}$ by $\bk^iL_k$, which grows with $\vee$. We therefore use $\mathfrak B$, Eq.~\eqref{eq:Bfull}.
\begin{table}[H]
\centering\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}c c c c@{}}
$\vee$ & $\mathfrak B^1_\xi$ (\texttt{main.tex}, $\xi$ integral) & $\mathfrak B^1$ (with $-\bk^1L_k$) & $\mathfrak B^1_{\rm msg}$ (your message)\\ \hline
$10^{2}$ & $0.306467$ & $0.304224$ & $0.309890$\\
$10^{2}$ & $11.741657$ & $11.718828$ & $9.624992$\\
$10^{4}$ & $8.028061$ & $8.028089$ & $3.494700$\\
$10^{4}$ & $-2.180515$ & $-2.180523$ & $-1.209622$\\
\end{tabular}
\caption{The bracket of Group I, Row II at random transverse momenta.}
\label{tab:xi}
\end{table}
The extra term contains no $\Lambda$. It therefore does not change the terms proportional to $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ (Parts~\ref{part:three} and~\ref{part:jimwlk}). It does change the terms proportional to $\log(\vee/k^+)$ (Part~\ref{part:bfkl}).

\subsection{Splitting the bracket into \texorpdfstring{$\log(\vee/\Lambda)$}{log(vee/Lambda)}, \texorpdfstring{$\log(\vee/k^+)$}{log(vee/k+)} and the rest}\label{sec:ft-split}
For $\vee\gg k^+$ (at fixed $k^+$ and transverse momenta) the three logarithms are, step by step:
\begin{align}
\ell_1&=-\log\Big(\frac{\vee\bk^2}{k^+\bp^2}+1\Big)=-\log\frac{\vee}{k^+}-\log\frac{\bk^2}{\bp^2}-\log\Big(1+\frac{k^+\bp^2}{\vee\bk^2}\Big)=-L_k+\log\frac{\bp^2}{\bk^2}+O\Big(\frac{k^+}{\vee}\Big),\notag\\
\ell_2&=\log\Big[\frac{\vee}{k^+}\frac{\bk^2}{\bq^2}\Big(1+\frac{k^+}{\vee}\Big(\frac{\bq^2}{\bk^2}-1\Big)\Big)\Big]=L_k+\log\frac{\bk^2}{\bq^2}+O\Big(\frac{k^+}{\vee}\Big),\notag\\
\ell_3&=O\Big(\frac{k^+}{\vee}\Big).
\end{align}
Insert these and $C^i=\bk^i+D^i$ into Eq.~\eqref{eq:Bfull}:
\begin{align}
\mathfrak B^i={}&\big[-L_\Lambda+L_k\big]E^i-2\bk^iL_\Lambda-2\bk^i\Big[-L_k+\log\frac{\bp^2}{\bk^2}\Big]+\big(\bk^i+D^i\big)\Big[L_k+\log\frac{\bk^2}{\bq^2}\Big]-D^iL_\Lambda-\bk^iL_k+O\Big(\frac{k^+}{\vee}\Big).
\end{align}
Collecting the coefficients of $L_\Lambda$ and $L_k$:
\begin{align}
L_\Lambda:&\quad -E^i-2\bk^i-D^i\;\equiv\;A^i,\notag\\
L_k:&\quad +E^i+2\bk^i+\bk^i+D^i-\bk^i=E^i+2\bk^i+D^i\;\equiv\;B^i=-A^i,\notag\\
\text{rest}:&\quad -2\bk^i\log\frac{\bp^2}{\bk^2}+\big(\bk^i+D^i\big)\log\frac{\bk^2}{\bq^2}\;\equiv\;R^i.
\end{align}
Hence
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\;\mathfrak B^i=A^i\,L_\Lambda-A^i\,L_k+R^i+O\Big(\frac{k^+}{\vee}\Big)=A^i\log\frac{k^+}{\Lambda}+R^i+O\Big(\frac{k^+}{\vee}\Big),\qquad A^i=-E^i-2\bk^i-D^i .\;}
\label{eq:Bsplit}
\end{equation}
Table~\ref{tab:largev} checks Eq.~\eqref{eq:Bsplit} numerically: the difference falls like $1/\vee$. With the bracket of the message, the coefficient of $L_k$ would be $B^i_{\rm msg}=B^i+\bk^i$, i.e.\ $A^i+B^i_{\rm msg}=\bk^i\neq0$.
\begin{table}[H]
\centering\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}c c c c@{}}
point & $\vee/k^+$ & $\max_i|\mathfrak B^i-(A^iL_\Lambda+B^iL_k+R^i)|$ & $\max_i|\mathfrak B^i|$\\ \hline
1 & $10^{2}$ & $8.0\times10^{-4}$ & $25.74$\\
1 & $10^{4}$ & $8.2\times10^{-6}$ & $25.74$\\
1 & $10^{6}$ & $8.2\times10^{-8}$ & $25.74$\\
2 & $10^{2}$ & $2.7\times10^{-1}$ & $18.65$\\
2 & $10^{4}$ & $2.8\times10^{-3}$ & $18.91$\\
2 & $10^{6}$ & $2.8\times10^{-5}$ & $18.92$\\
3 & $10^{2}$ & $6.1\times10^{-3}$ & $17.11$\\
3 & $10^{4}$ & $6.2\times10^{-5}$ & $17.12$\\
3 & $10^{6}$ & $6.2\times10^{-7}$ & $17.12$\\
\end{tabular}
\caption{The split \eqref{eq:Bsplit} at random momenta ($\Lambda/k^+=10^{-3}$).}
\label{tab:largev}
\end{table}

\paragraph{What the split means.} $A^i$ is the coefficient of $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$, $B^i$ that of $\log(\vee/k^+)$. Their sum $A^i+B^i$ is the coefficient of $\log\vee$, which comes from virtual gluons much harder than the measured one ($p^+\gg k^+$). As written, $A^i+B^i=0$: in your notes the large logarithm of this row is $\log(k^+/\Lambda)$, i.e.\ the virtual gluon is always softer than the measured one. Sec.~\ref{sec:alpha} shows that this is not quite right for the part $-2\bk^i$ of $A^i$ (fix F4).

\paragraph{The earlier input was complete.} \texttt{Evolution\_three\_rho.tex}, which the three-$\rho$ analysis used, has for this row exactly $-L_\Lambda\big[E^i+2\bk^i+D^i\big]=A^iL_\Lambda$. The full bracket adds only terms with $L_k$ and terms without large logarithms, and none of them contains $\Lambda$. So no $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ term was missing. The Fourier transform below is the one used before (App.~A of \texttt{ThreeRho\_to\_TwoRho}), now with every step written out.

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{The Fourier transform, step by step}\label{sec:ft-steps}
For a momentum function $X^i(\bk,\bp)$ define its transform
\begin{equation}
F[X]^i(w;x,y)\equiv\int d^2\bk\,d^2\bp\;e^{i\bk\cdot(w-y)}\,e^{i\bp\cdot(y-x)}\,\frac{X^i(\bk,\bp)}{\bk^2\bp^2}.
\end{equation}
By Eq.~\eqref{eq:Bsplit} we need $F[A]$; then $F[B]=-F[A]$. The rest $R$ is discussed in Sec.~\ref{sec:ft-rest}.

\subsection{Only the part odd under \texorpdfstring{$x\leftrightarrow y$}{x<->y} contributes}\label{sec:ft-odd}
Call the two colour structures $O_1(x,y)=U^{ah}(w)f^{hbc}\{\rho^b(x),\rho^c(y)\}$ and $O_2(x,y)=f^{abc}U^{bd}(x)U^{ce}(y)\{\rho^d(x),\rho^e(y)\}$.
\begin{itemize}
\item $O_1(y,x)=U^{ah}(w)f^{hbc}\{\rho^b(y),\rho^c(x)\}$. Rename the dummy indices $b\leftrightarrow c$: $U^{ah}(w)f^{hcb}\{\rho^c(y),\rho^b(x)\}$. The anticommutator is symmetric and $f^{hcb}=-f^{hbc}$, so $O_1(y,x)=-O_1(x,y)$.
\item $O_2(y,x)=f^{abc}U^{bd}(y)U^{ce}(x)\{\rho^d(y),\rho^e(x)\}$. Rename $b\leftrightarrow c$, $d\leftrightarrow e$: $f^{acb}U^{ce}(y)U^{bd}(x)\{\rho^e(y),\rho^d(x)\}=-O_2(x,y)$.
\end{itemize}
Write any kernel as $F=F_{\rm odd}+F_{\rm even}$ with $F_{\rm odd/even}(x,y)=\frac12[F(x,y)\mp F(y,x)]$. Renaming the integration variables $x\leftrightarrow y$ gives $\int_{x,y}F_{\rm even}O=\int_{x,y}F_{\rm even}(y,x)O(y,x)=-\int_{x,y}F_{\rm even}O$, so this integral vanishes. Hence only $F_{\rm odd}$ contributes:
\begin{equation}
\int_{x,y}F(x,y)\,O_{1,2}(x,y)=\int_{x,y}F_{\rm odd}(x,y)\,O_{1,2}(x,y).
\end{equation}
(In momentum space $x\leftrightarrow y$ is $\bp\leftrightarrow\bq$: the phase $e^{i\bk\cdot w}e^{-i\bp\cdot x}e^{-i\bq\cdot y}$ goes into $e^{i\bk\cdot w}e^{-i\bq\cdot x}e^{-i\bp\cdot y}$.)

\subsection{Three basic transforms}\label{sec:ft-basic}
\paragraph{(T2) The propagator.} $\int d^2\bk\,e^{i\bk\cdot X}/\bk^2$ diverges at small $\bk$. With a small mass $m$ as regulator, do the angular integral first, $\int_0^{2\pi}d\theta\,e^{i|\bk||X|\cos\theta}=2\pi J_0(|\bk||X|)$, and then use $\int_0^\infty dk\,k\,J_0(k|X|)/(k^2+m^2)=K_0(m|X|)$:
\begin{equation}
\int d^2\bk\,\frac{e^{i\bk\cdot X}}{\bk^2+m^2}=2\pi K_0(m|X|)=-\pi\log X^2+C_0+O(m^2X^2),\qquad C_0=-2\pi\Big[\log\frac m2+\gamma_E\Big].
\tag{T2}
\end{equation}
Here we used $K_0(t)=-\log(t/2)-\gamma_E+O(t^2)$. The constant $C_0$ does not depend on $X$; it cancels in all results below.
\paragraph{(T1) The emission kernel.} Since $\bk^ie^{i\bk\cdot X}=-i\,\partial^i_Xe^{i\bk\cdot X}$, and $\partial^i\log X^2=2X^i/X^2$,
\begin{equation}
\int d^2\bk\,e^{i\bk\cdot X}\,\frac{\bk^i}{\bk^2}=-i\,\partial^i_X\big(-\pi\log X^2+C_0\big)=2\pi i\,\frac{X^i}{X^2}=2\pi i\,\KK^i(X).
\tag{T1}
\end{equation}
Inverting the Fourier transform, $\KK^i(u)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int d^2\bk\,e^{i\bk\cdot u}\bk^i/\bk^2$ gives
\begin{equation}
\frac{\bk^i}{\bk^2}=\frac{2\pi i}{(2\pi)^2}\int d^2u\;e^{-i\bk\cdot u}\,\KK^i(u)=\frac{i}{2\pi}\int d^2u\;e^{-i\bk\cdot u}\,\KK^i(u).
\tag{T1$'$}
\end{equation}
\paragraph{(T3) The soft-gluon kernel.} With $K(a,b,z)\equiv\KK(a-z)\cdot\KK(b-z)$, write both $\KK$'s with (T1), and use $\int d^2z\,e^{-i(\bk+\bk')\cdot z}=(2\pi)^2\delta^{(2)}(\bk+\bk')$:
\begin{align}
\int_zK(a,b,z)&=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^2}\int d^2\bk\,d^2\bk'\,\frac{\bk\cdot\bk'}{\bk^2\bk'^2}\,e^{i\bk\cdot a+i\bk'\cdot b}\int_ze^{-i(\bk+\bk')\cdot z}
=\frac{(2\pi)^2}{(2\pi i)^2}\int d^2\bk\,\frac{\bk\cdot(-\bk)}{\bk^2\,\bk^2}\,e^{i\bk\cdot(a-b)}\notag\\
&=\int d^2\bk\,\frac{e^{i\bk\cdot(a-b)}}{\bk^2}=-\pi\log(a-b)^2+C_0 .
\tag{T3}
\end{align}
So $\int d^2\bk\,e^{i\bk\cdot X}/\bk^2$ is the soft-gluon kernel integrated over the gluon position, with the same infrared constant.

\subsection{Partial fractions}\label{sec:ft-pf}
$A^i=-(E^i+D^i)-2\bk^i$, and
\begin{equation}
E^i+D^i=\frac{\bk^2\bp^i-(\bk\cdot\bp)\bk^i+\bp^2\bk^i+\bk^2\bp^i}{\bq^2}=\frac{2\bk^2\bp^i+(\bp^2-\bk\cdot\bp)\,\bk^i}{\bq^2}=\frac{2\bk^2\bp^i-(\bp\cdot\bq)\,\bk^i}{\bq^2},
\end{equation}
because $\bp^2-\bk\cdot\bp=-\bp\cdot(\bk-\bp)=-\bp\cdot\bq$. Dividing by $\bk^2\bp^2$:
\begin{equation}
\frac{A^i}{\bk^2\bp^2}=\underbrace{-\frac{2\bp^i}{\bq^2\,\bp^2}}_{(a)}\;+\;\underbrace{\frac{(\bp\cdot\bq)\,\bk^i}{\bq^2\,\bk^2\,\bp^2}}_{(b)}\;\underbrace{-\;\frac{2\bk^i}{\bk^2\,\bp^2}}_{(c)} .
\label{eq:pf}
\end{equation}
Terms (a) and (b) come from $E^i+D^i$ (the pole at $\bq=0$); term (c) comes from $-2\bk^i$. (Checked with \texttt{sympy}.)

\subsection{The three transforms}\label{sec:ft-three}
\paragraph{Term (c).} It factorizes. By (T1) and (T3),
\begin{equation}
F[(c)]^i=-2\int d^2\bk\,e^{i\bk\cdot(w-y)}\frac{\bk^i}{\bk^2}\int d^2\bp\,\frac{e^{i\bp\cdot(y-x)}}{\bp^2}=-2\cdot2\pi i\,\KK^i(w-y)\int_zK(x,y,z)=4\pi i\,\KK^i(y-w)\int_zK(x,y,z).
\end{equation}
\paragraph{Term (a).} Change variables from $(\bk,\bp)$ to $(\bq,\bp)$, $\bk=\bq+\bp$ (Jacobian 1). The phase becomes
\begin{equation}
\bk\cdot(w-y)+\bp\cdot(y-x)=\bq\cdot(w-y)+\bp\cdot(w-y)+\bp\cdot(y-x)=\bq\cdot(w-y)+\bp\cdot(w-x),
\end{equation}
so
\begin{equation}
F[(a)]^i=-2\int d^2\bp\,e^{i\bp\cdot(w-x)}\frac{\bp^i}{\bp^2}\int d^2\bq\,\frac{e^{i\bq\cdot(w-y)}}{\bq^2}=-2\cdot2\pi i\,\KK^i(w-x)\int_zK(w,y,z)=4\pi i\,\KK^i(x-w)\int_zK(w,y,z).
\end{equation}
\paragraph{Term (b).} Write $\bk^i/\bk^2$ with (T1$'$) and change variables to $(\bq,\bp)$. The phase becomes $\bq\cdot(w-y-u)+\bp\cdot(w-x-u)$, and the $\bp$ and $\bq$ integrals factorize:
\begin{align}
F[(b)]^i&=\frac{i}{2\pi}\int d^2u\,\KK^i(u)\int d^2\bp\,e^{i\bp\cdot(w-x-u)}\frac{\bp^j}{\bp^2}\int d^2\bq\,e^{i\bq\cdot(w-y-u)}\frac{\bq^j}{\bq^2}\notag\\
&=\frac{i}{2\pi}(2\pi i)^2\int d^2u\,\KK^i(u)\,\KK(w-u-x)\cdot\KK(w-u-y)\notag\\
&=-2\pi i\int_z\KK^i(w-z)\,K(x,y,z)\qquad(z\equiv w-u).
\end{align}

\subsection{The result}\label{sec:ft-result}
Now take the part odd under $x\leftrightarrow y$:
\begin{itemize}
\item (b) is even, because $K(x,y,z)=K(y,x,z)$. It drops out.
\item (a): $\frac12\big[F[(a)](x,y)-F[(a)](y,x)\big]=2\pi i\big[\KK^i(x-w)\int_zK(w,y,z)-\KK^i(y-w)\int_zK(w,x,z)\big]$.
\item (c): $\frac12\big[F[(c)](x,y)-F[(c)](y,x)\big]=-2\pi i\big[\KK^i(x-w)-\KK^i(y-w)\big]\int_zK(x,y,z)$.
\end{itemize}
Therefore
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\;F[A]^i_{\rm odd}=2\pi i\,\mathfrak F^i(w;x,y),\qquad \mathfrak F^i=\mathfrak F^i_\beta+\mathfrak F^i_\alpha\;}
\label{eq:Fresult}
\end{equation}
with
\begin{align}
\mathfrak F^i_\beta(w;x,y)&=\KK^i(x-w)\int_zK(w,y,z)-\KK^i(y-w)\int_zK(w,x,z),\notag\\
\mathfrak F^i_\alpha(w;x,y)&=-\big[\KK^i(x-w)-\KK^i(y-w)\big]\int_zK(x,y,z).
\label{eq:Fab}
\end{align}
$\mathfrak F_\beta$ comes from $E^i+D^i$ (the pole at $\bq=0$): the soft gluon at $z$ connects the measured gluon at $w$ with a charge. $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ comes from $-2\bk^i$: the soft gluon connects the two charges $x$ and $y$.
\paragraph{Infrared finiteness.} By (T3) each $\int_zK$ is $-\pi\log(\cdot)^2+C_0$. In the sum $\mathfrak F_\beta+\mathfrak F_\alpha$ the constant multiplies $\KK^i(x-w)(C_0-C_0)-\KK^i(y-w)(C_0-C_0)=0$, so
\begin{equation}
\mathfrak F^i(w;x,y)=-\pi\Big[\KK^i(x-w)\log\frac{(w-y)^2}{(x-y)^2}-\KK^i(y-w)\log\frac{(w-x)^2}{(x-y)^2}\Big],
\label{eq:Flog}
\end{equation}
which is finite. In the rest of this note all kernels are kept as integrands in the soft-gluon position $z$; the large-$z$ behaviour of the individual pieces cancels in the sums (JIMWLK$\otimes$LO and BFKL$\otimes$LO).

\paragraph{Prefactor.} With Eq.~\eqref{eq:Fresult} the prefactor of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ft-term} becomes
\begin{equation}
-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^3}\frac{g^4}{64\pi^6}\frac1{k^+}\cdot2\pi i=-\frac i2\cdot\frac{1}{(2\pi)^3}\frac{g^4}{16\pi^5}\frac1{k^+},
\end{equation}
i.e.\ $-\frac i2\NN$ for the $L_\Lambda$ term and, because $B^i=-A^i$, $+\frac i2\NP$ for the $L_k$ term (units of Sec.~\ref{sec:conv}).

\paragraph{Group I, Row II in coordinate space.} Collecting,
\begin{align}
\frac{d^3N}{d^3k}\bigg|_{\rm I.II,\,3\rho}={}&\Big(-\frac i2\NN+\frac i2\NP\Big)\int e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}\,\KK^i(x'-w')\,\mathfrak F^i(w;x,y)\,\Big\{\notag\\
&\quad +\,U^{ab'}(x')\,f^{hbc}\,U^{ah}(w)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\big\{\rho^{b}(x),\rho^{c}(y)\big\}
-\,U^{ab'}(x')\,f^{abc}\,U^{bd}(x)\,U^{ce}(y)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\big\{\rho^{d}(x),\rho^{e}(y)\big\}\notag\\
&\quad -\,U^{ab'}(w')\,f^{hbc}\,U^{ah}(w)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\big\{\rho^{b}(x),\rho^{c}(y)\big\}
+\,U^{ab'}(w')\,f^{abc}\,U^{bd}(x)\,U^{ce}(y)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\big\{\rho^{d}(x),\rho^{e}(y)\big\}\Big\}\notag\\
&+(\text{finite, Sec.~\ref{sec:ft-rest}})+{\rm c.c.}
\label{eq:RowIIcoord}
\end{align}
Since $-\frac i2(\NN-\NP)\propto\log(\vee/\Lambda)-\log(\vee/k^+)=\log(k^+/\Lambda)$, the whole row is proportional to $\log(k^+/\Lambda)$, as found in Eq.~\eqref{eq:Bsplit}. The sub-terms are numbered 1--4 in the order shown: 1 and 3 contain $U(w)\mathbb A^{(3)}$, 2 and 4 the rotated $\bar{\mathbb A}^{(3)}$. The $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ part is exactly the input of the three-$\rho$ analysis (Part~\ref{part:three} and App.~\ref{app:reorder}).

With the bracket of your message, the $L_k$ term would carry $\mathfrak F+\frac12\mathfrak F_\alpha$ instead of $\mathfrak F$ (the extra $+\bk^iL_k$ transforms like term (c) with weight $-\frac12$).

\subsection{The rest \texorpdfstring{$R^i$}{R}}\label{sec:ft-rest}
\begin{equation}
R^i=-2\bk^i\log\frac{\bp^2}{\bk^2}+\big(\bk^i+D^i\big)\log\frac{\bk^2}{\bq^2},\qquad
\frac{R^i}{\bk^2\bp^2}=-\frac{2\bk^i}{\bk^2\bp^2}\log\frac{\bp^2}{\bk^2}+\Big[\frac{\bk^i}{\bk^2\bp^2}+\frac{\bk^i}{\bq^2\bk^2}+\frac{\bp^i}{\bq^2\bp^2}\Big]\log\frac{\bk^2}{\bq^2}.
\end{equation}
(The second form uses $D^i/(\bk^2\bp^2)=\bk^i/(\bq^2\bk^2)+\bp^i/(\bq^2\bp^2)$.) $R^i$ contains neither $\vee$, nor $\Lambda$, nor $k^+$. It is part of the finite NLO correction and does not enter the evolution. Only its part odd under $\bp\leftrightarrow\bq$ contributes. Its transform involves $\log^2$ of distances and three-point integrals that do not factorize; as \texttt{main.tex} does for its finite terms, we keep it in momentum space:
\begin{equation}
\frac{d^3N}{d^3k}\bigg|_{\rm I.II,\,finite}=-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^3}\frac{g^4f^{abc}}{64\pi^6}\frac1{k^+}\int e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}\int d^2\bk\,d^2\bp\int_{x,y}e^{i\bk\cdot(w-y)}e^{i\bp\cdot(y-x)}\,\frac{\KK^i(x'-w')\,R^i}{\bk^2\bp^2}\,\big[\dots\big]\big[\dots\big]+{\rm c.c.},
\end{equation}
with the colour brackets of Eq.~\eqref{eq:ft-term}.

%------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{The \texorpdfstring{$\alpha$}{alpha} part is an operator-ordering commutator}\label{sec:alpha}
The three-$\rho$ terms proportional to $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ cancel only if the part $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ is removed from the $U(w)\mathbb A^{(3)}$ term of Row II (fix F4, Part~\ref{part:three}). Here we show what this part is.

\paragraph{Ingredients (\texttt{main.tex}).} With $\rho^a(\bp)\equiv\int_xe^{-i\bp\cdot x}\rho^a(x)$, the charge algebra reads
\begin{equation}
[\rho^a(\bk),\rho^b(\bp)]=\int_{x,y}e^{-i\bk\cdot x-i\bp\cdot y}\,if^{abc}\rho^c(x)\,\delta^{(2)}(x-y)=if^{abc}\rho^c(\bk+\bp).
\label{eq:commk}
\end{equation}
The LO amplitude and the normalization are (Eqs.~(A1coeffM), (Ncoeffpos) of \texttt{main.tex}; for $\mathbb N$ use (T3))
\begin{align}
\mathbb A^{(1)a}_{2i}(k^+,\bk)&=-\frac{\sqrt2\,g\,\bk^i\rho^a(-\bk)}{\sqrt{k^+}\,\bk^2},\\
\mathbb N^{(2)}&=-\frac{g^2}{(2\pi)^3}L_\Lambda\int_{x,y,z}K(x,y,z)\,\rho^b(x)\rho^b(y)=-\frac{g^2}{8\pi^3}L_\Lambda\int d^2\bp\,\frac{\rho^b(\bp)\rho^b(-\bp)}{\bp^2}\equiv L_\Lambda\,n .
\end{align}
$\mathbb A^{(3)a}_{2i}$ of \texttt{main.tex} contains a three-charge term and the $f\{\rho,\rho\}$ term whose bracket is $\mathfrak B^i$:
\begin{align}
T_3&=\int d^2\bp\,\frac{g^3\,\rho^b(\bp)\rho^b(-\bp)\rho^a(-\bk)\,\bk^i}{4\sqrt2\pi^3\,\bk^2\bp^2\sqrt{k^+}}\,L_\Lambda,\\
T_f&=\int d^2\bp\,\frac{ig^3f^{abc}\{\rho^b(-\bp),\rho^c(\bp-\bk)\}}{8\sqrt2\pi^3\,\bp^2\bk^2\sqrt{k^+}}\,\mathfrak B^i .
\end{align}
\paragraph{Step 1: the three-charge term is $\mathbb N^{(2)}\mathbb A^{(1)}$.} Multiplying,
\begin{equation}
\mathbb N^{(2)}\mathbb A^{(1)a}_{2i}=\Big(-\frac{g^2L_\Lambda}{8\pi^3}\Big)\Big(-\frac{\sqrt2\,g\,\bk^i}{\sqrt{k^+}\bk^2}\Big)\int d^2\bp\,\frac{\rho^b(\bp)\rho^b(-\bp)\rho^a(-\bk)}{\bp^2}
=\frac{\sqrt2}{8}\,\frac{g^3\bk^iL_\Lambda}{\pi^3\sqrt{k^+}\bk^2}\int d^2\bp\,\frac{\rho^b(\bp)\rho^b(-\bp)\rho^a(-\bk)}{\bp^2}=T_3,
\end{equation}
since $\sqrt2/8=1/(4\sqrt2)$.
\paragraph{Step 2: a commutator.} By Eq.~\eqref{eq:commk},
\begin{align}
\big[\rho^b(\bp)\rho^b(-\bp),\rho^a(-\bk)\big]&=\rho^b(\bp)\big[\rho^b(-\bp),\rho^a(-\bk)\big]+\big[\rho^b(\bp),\rho^a(-\bk)\big]\rho^b(-\bp)\notag\\
&=if^{bac}\rho^b(\bp)\rho^c(-\bp-\bk)+if^{bac}\rho^c(\bp-\bk)\rho^b(-\bp).
\end{align}
Under $\int d^2\bp/\bp^2$ we may replace $\bp\to-\bp$ in the first term. Then both terms have the same arguments:
\begin{equation}
\int\frac{d^2\bp}{\bp^2}\big[\rho^b(\bp)\rho^b(-\bp),\rho^a(-\bk)\big]=\int\frac{d^2\bp}{\bp^2}\,if^{bac}\big\{\rho^b(-\bp),\rho^c(\bp-\bk)\big\}=-i\int\frac{d^2\bp}{\bp^2}\,f^{abc}\big\{\rho^b(-\bp),\rho^c(\bp-\bk)\big\}.
\end{equation}
\paragraph{Step 3: the $-2\bk^iL_\Lambda$ term of $\mathfrak B^i$ is $[\mathbb N^{(2)},\mathbb A^{(1)}]$.} With Step 2,
\begin{equation}
\big[\mathbb N^{(2)},\mathbb A^{(1)a}_{2i}\big]=\frac{\sqrt2\,g^3\bk^iL_\Lambda}{8\pi^3\sqrt{k^+}\bk^2}\int\frac{d^2\bp}{\bp^2}\big[\rho^b(\bp)\rho^b(-\bp),\rho^a(-\bk)\big]
=-\frac{ig^3\bk^iL_\Lambda}{4\sqrt2\pi^3\sqrt{k^+}\bk^2}\int\frac{d^2\bp}{\bp^2}f^{abc}\{\rho^b(-\bp),\rho^c(\bp-\bk)\}.
\end{equation}
This is $T_f$ with $\mathfrak B^i\to-2\bk^iL_\Lambda$, because $\frac{i}{8\sqrt2}\cdot(-2)=-\frac{i}{4\sqrt2}$. More generally, the part $-2\bk^i\,X$ of $\mathfrak B^i$ equals $X\,[n,\mathbb A^{(1)}_2]$ for any longitudinal factor $X$. In $\mathfrak B^i$ the terms of this type are $-2\bk^iL_\Lambda$ and $-2\bk^i(-L_k)$ from $\ell_1$ (the $\bk^i$ in $C^i$ and the extra $-\bk^iL_k$ cancel). So
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\;\mathbb A^{(3)}_2\supset L_\Lambda\,n\,\mathbb A^{(1)}_2+L_T\,\big[n,\mathbb A^{(1)}_2\big],\qquad L_T=\log\frac{k^+}{\Lambda}\;}
\label{eq:Awritten}
\end{equation}
and in coordinate space the commutator term is the $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ part of Row II: the soft gluon is exchanged between the two charges, $\int_zK(x,y,z)$, as in $n$. This identity was also checked with explicit charge matrices (SU(2) charges on three sites, random kernels): $[\sum K_{ij}\rho^b_i\rho^b_j,\sum c_l\rho^a_l]=-\frac i2\sum K_{ij}(c_j-c_i)f^{abc}\{\rho^b_i,\rho^c_j\}$ holds to $3.9\times10^{-16}$.

\paragraph{Step 4: what fix F4 does.} Split $L_\Lambda=L_T+L_P$ with $L_T=\log(k^+/\Lambda)$ (virtual gluons softer than the measured one) and $L_P=\log(\vee/k^+)=L_k$ (harder ones). Order the virtual factor in $p^+$: the soft part to the left of $\mathbb A^{(1)}$, the hard part to the right,
\begin{equation}
L_T\,n\,\mathbb A^{(1)}_2+L_P\,\mathbb A^{(1)}_2\,n=L_\Lambda\,n\,\mathbb A^{(1)}_2-L_P\,\big[n,\mathbb A^{(1)}_2\big].
\label{eq:Aordered}
\end{equation}
Eq.~\eqref{eq:Awritten} minus Eq.~\eqref{eq:Aordered} is $(L_T+L_P)[n,\mathbb A^{(1)}_2]=L_\Lambda[n,\mathbb A^{(1)}_2]$. So the $p^+$-ordered form is the form of the notes with the commutator removed from the coefficient of $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$, while the coefficient of $\log(\vee/k^+)$ is unchanged. In the cross section (sub-terms 1, 3 of Eq.~\eqref{eq:RowIIcoord}) this is exactly fix F4:
\begin{equation}
\text{written: }\big(\mathfrak F_\alpha+\mathfrak F_\beta\big)\log\frac{k^+}{\Lambda}\qquad\longrightarrow\qquad\text{F4: }\mathfrak F_\beta\log\frac{k^+}{\Lambda}-\mathfrak F_\alpha\log\frac{\vee}{k^+}.
\label{eq:F4meaning}
\end{equation}
The soft gluon between the two valence charges ($\mathfrak F_\alpha$) is a virtual correction of the projectile; with F4 it comes with the projectile interval $\log(\vee/k^+)$ and belongs to BFKL (Part~\ref{part:bfkl}). The soft gluon between the measured gluon and a charge ($\mathfrak F_\beta$) comes with the target interval $\log(k^+/\Lambda)$ and belongs to JIMWLK (Part~\ref{part:jimwlk}).

\paragraph{Where \texttt{main.tex} loses the order.} After the unitarity relations, \texttt{main.tex} sets $\mathbb N^{(2)}\mathbb A^{(1)}=\mathbb A^{(1)}\mathbb N^{(2)}$, arguing that $\mathbb A^{(1)}$ is linear in $\rho$. As operators they do not commute: by Step 3 their commutator is the $\alpha$ term itself. The equations of \texttt{main.tex} therefore do not fix where the virtual factor stands relative to $\mathbb A^{(1)}$. Three independent checks show that the ordered form \eqref{eq:Aordered} is the right one:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the symmetrized three-$\rho$ terms proportional to $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ cancel with it and do not cancel without it (Part~\ref{part:three});
\item the two-$\rho$ terms proportional to $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ then equal JIMWLK$\otimes$LO (Part~\ref{part:jimwlk});
\item the coefficient of $\log\vee$ then equals BFKL$\otimes$LO; in particular Row II contributes $-\mathfrak F_\alpha$ to it, as the independent region-$p^+\gg k^+$ computation requires (Part~\ref{part:bfkl}).
\end{enumerate}
The rotated term $\bar{\mathbb A}^{(3)}$ (sub-terms 2, 4) is correct as written; its $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ part is needed for the cancellation (networks $\alpha_3$, $\alpha_4$ of Part~\ref{part:three}).


%==========================================================================================
\part{The three-\texorpdfstring{$\rho$}{rho} terms proportional to \texorpdfstring{$\log(\vee/\Lambda)$}{log(vee/Lambda)}: the cancellation}\label{part:three}
%==========================================================================================
This part adds up the symmetrized parts $S(\rho\rho\rho)$ of all three-$\rho$ terms proportional to $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$. Group I, Row II enters with the coordinate-space kernel of Part~\ref{part:ft}: its four monomials a--d are the $z$ integrands of $\mathfrak F_\beta$ (a, c) and $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ (b, d). The step-by-step reordering of every term, which also gives the two-$\rho$ terms used in Part~\ref{part:jimwlk}, is in Apps.~\ref{R:sec:identity}--\ref{R:sec:summary}.

\section{Overview}\label{C:sec:answer}

\paragraph{Question.} Apps.~\ref{R:sec:identity}--\ref{R:sec:summary} reorder every three-$\rho$ term proportional to $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ into its fully symmetrized part $S(\rho\rho\rho)$ plus two-$\rho$ terms. Here we add up all the symmetrized parts:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the three-$\rho$ remainders of the four-$\rho$ terms, with two Wilson lines (\lab{A1}, \lab{A2}) and with three Wilson lines (\lab{B1}, \lab{B2}); no c.c.;
\item Group I, Rows II--V, with c.c.;
\item Group II, Row I (both terms), no c.c.;
\item Group III, Rows I, II, III, V, VI, with c.c.
\end{enumerate}
Does the sum vanish?

\paragraph{Answer.}
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{As written, no.} In common integration variables the three-$\rho$ terms split into 160 entries (one source, one sub-term, one kernel monomial, amplitude or c.c.). The entries fall into six kernel classes and 48 colour networks. 28 networks cancel exactly. 20 networks do not; each of them is left with $\pm2i\NN$ times one colour structure (Table~\ref{C:tab:summary}, Sec.~\ref{C:sec:networks}).
\item \textbf{Every left-over traces back to Group I.} Rows II and III of Group I contain terms with no partner, and one Group I term is missing:
\begin{itemize}
\item Row II: the part $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ of the two-charge kernel (soft gluon between the two charges $x$, $y$) in the term $U(w)\mathbb A^{(3)}$ (networks $\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\alpha'_1,\alpha'_2$);
\item Row III, part 2: the edge kernel $\Phi$ appears nowhere else (networks $\gamma_{1\ldots4}$, $\gamma'_{1\ldots4}$);
\item a missing term $\mathcal D$ (soft gluon between the measured gluon and a charge), Eq.~\eqref{C:eq:D} (networks $\beta_2,\beta_4,\beta_{11},\beta_{12}$ and $\beta'_2,\beta'_4,\beta'_{11},\beta'_{12}$).
\end{itemize}
The four-$\rho$ remainders, Group II and Group III are correct as written: they agree row by row with an independent leading-log computation (Sec.~\ref{C:sec:reference}).
\item \textbf{Two changes in Group I make everything cancel} (Sec.~\ref{C:sec:corrections}):
\begin{itemize}
\item[(1)] in Row II, the term $U^{ah}(w)f^{hbc}\{\rho^b(x),\rho^c(y)\}$ keeps only the part $\mathfrak F_\beta$ of the kernel;
\item[(2)] in Row III, part 2 (the $\Phi$ terms) is replaced by $\mathcal D$.
\end{itemize}
After them all 40 remaining networks cancel with exact coefficients (Sec.~\ref{C:sec:after}), and the corrected Group I agrees with the independent computation piece by piece.
\item \textbf{Independent numerical test.} We evaluate the total of all symmetrized three-$\rho$ terms at random points without any bookkeeping. As written, it is 5--40\% of the sum of the absolute values of the terms; after the two changes, $10^{-16}$ (Sec.~\ref{C:sec:pointwise}).
\item \textbf{Convention.} All of this uses $U^{bc}=U^{cb}$, as the notes do. Sec.~\ref{C:sec:Usym} explains what changes for a non-symmetric $U$.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Method}\label{C:sec:method}

\subsection{Common variables and the six kernel classes}\label{C:sec:classes}

After symmetrization the charges are commuting variables. The three charge positions and the soft-gluon position are integrated, so they may be renamed freely. In every kernel monomial
\begin{itemize}
\item one charge is tied to $w$ by its LO emission kernel $\KK(\,\cdot-w)$: we call its position $x$;
\item one charge is tied to $w'$ by $\KK(\,\cdot-w')$: we call it $x'$;
\item the third charge is called $y$, and the soft gluon $z$.
\end{itemize}
Before this, the measured-gluon positions are given their standard names: in Row V of Group I (first term $z\to w$, $x\to z$; second term $x\to w$) and in Rows III, V, VI of Group III ($y'\to w'$). In a c.c.\ entry we first swap $w\leftrightarrow w'$, Eq.~\eqref{eq:cc}. After the renaming every kernel monomial is one of
\begin{align}
K_\alpha&=\KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\;\KK(x-z)\cdot\KK(y-z) &&\text{soft gluon between the charges }x,\ y,\notag\\
K_{\alpha'}&=\KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\;\KK(x'-z)\cdot\KK(y-z) &&\text{soft gluon between the charges }x',\ y,\notag\\
K_\beta&=\KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\;\KK(w-z)\cdot\KK(y-z) &&\text{soft gluon between the measured gluon at }w\text{ and }y,\notag\\
K_{\beta'}&=\KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\;\KK(w'-z)\cdot\KK(y-z) &&\text{soft gluon between the measured gluon at }w'\text{ and }y,\notag\\
K_\gamma&=\KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(y-z)\;\Phi_w(x) &&\text{Row III of Group I, part 2,}\notag\\
K_{\gamma'}&=\KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(y-z)\;\Phi_{w'}(x') &&\text{its c.c.}
\label{C:eq:classes}
\end{align}
Bringing a monomial to this form may require $\KK(a-b)=-\KK(b-a)$; that sign is called $\varepsilon$ and is included in the coefficients. $\Phi_w(x)$ is the function $\Phi(x)$ of Row III (it depends on $x$, $z$ and $w$); $\Phi_{w'}$ is the same with $w\to w'$. With $A=x-w$, $B=x-z$, so that $z-w=A-B$, one has $2\big[(z-w)\cdot(x-z)A^2-(z-w)\cdot(x-w)B^2\big]=2A\cdot B(A^2+B^2)-4A^2B^2=(A^2-B^2)^2-(z-w)^2(A^2+B^2)$, and therefore
\begin{equation}
\Phi_w(x)=\frac{1}{(z-w)^2}\bigg[\frac{2(z-w)\cdot(x-z)\,(x-w)^2-2(z-w)\cdot(x-w)\,(x-z)^2}{\big[(x-w)^2-(x-z)^2\big]^2}-1\bigg]=-\frac{(x-w)^2+(x-z)^2}{\big[(x-w)^2-(x-z)^2\big]^2}.
\label{C:eq:Phi}
\end{equation}

\subsection{Colour networks}\label{C:sec:colour}
After the renaming, the colour structure of each entry is simplified with (U1) where possible and its dummy colour indices are renamed $a,b,c,\dots$. Inside one class all entries carry the same kernel. They can therefore only cancel if their colour structures are equal up to a sign. We call each distinct colour structure a \emph{network}. Two structures belong to the same network if they agree after (i) renaming dummy colour indices, (ii) using $f^{abc}=-f^{bac}$ etc., and (iii) using $U^{bc}=U^{cb}$.

\emph{Example} (network $\alpha_1$). Entry \lab{GII.I1.1b} has the structure $M=f^{abc}U^{de}(w')U^{da}(w)\rho^e(x')\rho^b(y)\rho^c(x)$. Rename $d\leftrightarrow e$ and use $U^{ed}(w')=U^{de}(w')$, $U^{ea}(w)=U^{ae}(w)$: $M=f^{abc}U^{de}(w')U^{ae}(w)\rho^d(x')\rho^b(y)\rho^c(x)$. Rename $a\leftrightarrow b$ and use $f^{bac}=-f^{abc}$ and $U^{be}=U^{eb}$: $M=-f^{abc}U^{de}(w')U^{eb}(w)\rho^d(x')\rho^a(y)\rho^c(x)$. This is $-1$ times the structure of entry \lab{A1.1}, the representative of $\alpha_1$. Hence \lab{GII.I1.1b} enters $\alpha_1$ with sign $-1$.

\emph{How the computer does it.} The sign is fixed by evaluating each structure for random symmetric adjoint $U$'s and random charges. Two independent random sets are used, and the ratio to the representative must be exactly $+1$ or $-1$ in both. Structures that are not equal give ratios that are not $\pm1$.

\subsection{Criterion}\label{C:sec:criterion}
The sum of all symmetrized three-$\rho$ terms is
\begin{equation}
\NN\int e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}\sum_{\text{classes }K}K\sum_{\text{networks }C\in K}\Big(\sum_{\text{entries }e\in C}c_e\,s_e\Big)\,C,
\end{equation}
where $c_e$ is the coefficient of entry $e$ (in units of $\NN$) and $s_e=\pm1$ its sign relative to the network. It vanishes if every network sum $\sum_e c_es_e$ vanishes. Conversely, a non-zero network sum is a genuine left-over. The six kernels are different functions of $x,x',y,z$. In the $\gamma$ networks with $U(z)$ the $z$-integral cannot even be done, and the kernel $\Phi$ is not integrable at all (Sec.~\ref{C:sec:diagnosis}), so the $\gamma$ entries cannot be traded for other classes by integrating. The networks are independent functions of the Wilson lines and charges. Sec.~\ref{C:sec:pointwise} confirms the conclusions pointwise, without using classes or networks.


\section{Every term in common variables}\label{C:sec:entries}

For every source we list the input (prefactor, factor $1$ or $2$ from the symmetrization, c.c., phase, kernel monomials, sub-terms), the renaming of each kernel monomial, and the resulting entries. Entry labels are \lab{source.n[m][*]}: sub-term $n$, kernel monomial $m$ (when there is more than one), and \lab{*} for the c.c. The coefficient of an entry, in units of $\NN$, is
\begin{equation}
c_e=(\text{prefactor})^{(*)}\times(1\text{ or }2)\times(\text{sign of sub-term }n)\times(\text{coefficient of monomial }m)\times\varepsilon,
\end{equation}
where the prefactor is complex conjugated for a c.c.\ entry. The colour structure is the sub-term's colour factor and charges, written in the common variables.

\subsubsection{\lab{A1}: Two Wilson lines, first term}\label{C:src:A1}

\subsubsection{\lab{GI.III}: Group I, Row III ($\bar{\mathbb B}_3^\dagger\mathbb A$)}\label{C:src:GI.III}
Prefactor $i\NN$; ordered product $\rho\rho\rho\to S(\rho\rho\rho)$, factor $1$; with c.c.; phase $e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}$.
$\Phi(x)$ is the edge kernel of the notes (it depends on $x$, $z$ and $w$); monomial b is part 2 of the row.
Kernel $K=\sum$ of the monomials
\begin{center}\small\begin{tabular}{@{}l >{$}l<{$}@{}}
a: & \KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(x-w)\,\KK(x-z)\cdot\KK(y-z)\\
b: & 2\,\KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(y-z)\,\Phi(x)\\
\end{tabular}\end{center}
The symmetrized term (commuting charges):
\begin{align*}
&i\NN\int e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}\,K\,\Big\{\\
&\qquad (1)\ \ +\,f^{abd}\,U^{ab'}(x')\,U^{de}(x)\,U^{bc}(z)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{e}(x)\,\rho^{c}(y)\\
&\qquad (2)\ \ -\,f^{abd}\,U^{ab'}(x')\,U^{de}(x)\,U^{bc}(y)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{e}(x)\,\rho^{c}(y)\\
&\qquad (3)\ \ -\,f^{abd}\,U^{ab'}(w')\,U^{de}(x)\,U^{bc}(z)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{e}(x)\,\rho^{c}(y)\\
&\qquad (4)\ \ +\,f^{abd}\,U^{ab'}(w')\,U^{de}(x)\,U^{bc}(y)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{e}(x)\,\rho^{c}(y)\Big\}+{\rm c.c.}
\end{align*}
\emph{Renaming to common variables} (all replacements simultaneous; $\varepsilon$ is the sign from $\KK(-X)=-\KK(X)$):
\begin{center}\small\begin{tabular}{@{}l l >{$}l<{$} >{$}l<{$} >{$}c<{$} >{$}c<{$}@{}}
mon. & term & \text{renaming} & \text{kernel in common variables} & \varepsilon & \text{class}\\ \hline
a & amp. & \text{none} & \KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(x-w)\,\KK(x-z)\cdot\KK(y-z) & + & \alpha\\
b & amp. & \text{none} & \KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(y-z)\,\Phi_{w}(x) & + & \gamma\\
a & c.c. & x'\to x,\ x\to x',\ w'\to w,\ w\to w' & \KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\,\KK(x'-z)\cdot\KK(y-z) & + & \alpha'\\
b & c.c. & x'\to x,\ x\to x',\ w'\to w,\ w\to w' & \KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(y-z)\,\Phi_{w'}(x') & + & \gamma'\\
\end{tabular}\end{center}

Prefactor $2i\NN$; ordered product $\rho\rho\rho\to S(\rho\rho\rho)$, factor $1$; with c.c.; phase $e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}$.
Kernel $K=\sum$ of the monomials

b: & \tfrac12\,\KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\,\KK(x-z)\cdot\KK(y-z)\\
\end{tabular}\end{center}

\end{align*}

\subsubsection{\lab{GI.V1}: Group I, Row V, first term (measured gluon at $z$)}\label{C:src:GI.V1}
Prefactor $-\tfrac{i}{2}\NN$; $\rho\{\rho,\rho\}\to 2S(\rho\rho\rho)$, factor $2$; with c.c.; phase $e^{-ik\cdot(w'-z)}$.
The measured gluon of the amplitude sits at $z$ and the soft gluon at $x$; we first rename $z\to w$, $x\to z$.
Kernel: $K=\KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(y-z)\,\KK(z-x)\cdot\KK(y'-x)$.
The symmetrized term (commuting charges):
\begin{align*}
&-i\NN\int e^{-ik\cdot(w'-z)}\,K\,\Big\{\\
&\qquad (1)\ \ +\,f^{adc}\,U^{ab'}(x')\,U^{db}(x)\,U^{ce}(z)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{e}(y)\,\rho^{b}(y')\\
&\qquad (2)\ \ -\,f^{abc}\,U^{ab'}(x')\,U^{cd}(y)\,U^{be}(y')\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{d}(y)\,\rho^{e}(y')\\
&\qquad (3)\ \ -\,f^{adc}\,U^{ab'}(w')\,U^{db}(x)\,U^{ce}(z)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{e}(y)\,\rho^{b}(y')\\
&\qquad (4)\ \ +\,f^{abc}\,U^{ab'}(w')\,U^{cd}(y)\,U^{be}(y')\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{d}(y)\,\rho^{e}(y')\Big\}+{\rm c.c.}
\end{align*}
\emph{Renaming to common variables} (all replacements simultaneous; $\varepsilon$ is the sign from $\KK(-X)=-\KK(X)$):
\begin{center}\small\begin{tabular}{@{}l l >{$}l<{$} >{$}l<{$} >{$}c<{$} >{$}c<{$}@{}}
mon. & term & \text{renaming} & \text{kernel in common variables} & \varepsilon & \text{class}\\ \hline
 & amp. & y\to x,\ y'\to y,\ x\to z,\ z\to w & \KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(x-w)\,\KK(w-z)\cdot\KK(y-z) & + & \beta\\
 & c.c. & x'\to x,\ y\to x',\ y'\to y,\ w'\to w,\ x\to z,\ z\to w' & \KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\,\KK(w'-z)\cdot\KK(y-z) & + & \beta'\\
\end{tabular}\end{center}
\emph{Entries} (coefficient in units of $\NN$; colour structure in common variables):

entry & \text{class} & \text{coeff.} & \text{colour structure} & \\ \hline\endhead

\subsubsection{\lab{GII.I1}: Group II, Row I, first term}\label{C:src:GII.I1}
Prefactor $-i\NN$; $\{\rho,\rho\}\rho\to 2S(\rho\rho\rho)$, factor $2$; no c.c.; phase $e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}$.
Kernel $K=\sum$ of the monomials
\begin{center}\small\begin{tabular}{@{}l >{$}l<{$}@{}}
a: & \KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\,\KK(z-w)\cdot\KK(y-z)\\
b: & \tfrac12\,\KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\,\KK(x-z)\cdot\KK(y-z)\\
\end{tabular}\end{center}
The symmetrized term (commuting charges):
\begin{align*}
&-2i\NN\int e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}\,K\,\Big\{\\
&\qquad (1)\ \ +\,f^{cbd}\,U^{ac'}(w')\,U^{ac}(w)\,\rho^{c'}(x')\,\rho^{b}(y)\,\rho^{d}(x)\\
&\qquad (2)\ \ -\,f^{acd}\,U^{ac'}(w')\,U^{bc}(z)\,U^{de}(x)\,\rho^{c'}(x')\,\rho^{b}(y)\,\rho^{e}(x)\\
&\qquad (3)\ \ -\,f^{cbd}\,U^{bc'}(z)\,U^{ad'}(x')\,U^{c'e'}(y)\,U^{ac}(w)\,\rho^{d'}(x')\,\rho^{e'}(y)\,\rho^{d}(x)\\
&\qquad (4)\ \ +\,f^{acd}\,U^{bc'}(z)\,U^{ad'}(x')\,U^{c'e'}(y)\,U^{bc}(z)\,U^{de}(x)\,\rho^{d'}(x')\,\rho^{e'}(y)\,\rho^{e}(x)\Big\}
\end{align*}
\emph{Renaming to common variables} (all replacements simultaneous; $\varepsilon$ is the sign from $\KK(-X)=-\KK(X)$):
\begin{center}\small\begin{tabular}{@{}l l >{$}l<{$} >{$}l<{$} >{$}c<{$} >{$}c<{$}@{}}
mon. & term & \text{renaming} & \text{kernel in common variables} & \varepsilon & \text{class}\\ \hline
a & amp. & \text{none} & \KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\,\KK(z-w)\cdot\KK(y-z) & - & \beta\\
b & amp. & \text{none} & \KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\,\KK(x-z)\cdot\KK(y-z) & + & \alpha\\
\end{tabular}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\lab{GII.I2}: Group II, Row I, second term}\label{C:src:GII.I2}

\subsubsection{\lab{GIII.VI}: Group III, Row VI}\label{C:src:GIII.VI}
Prefactor $-2i\NN$; ordered product $\rho\rho\rho\to S(\rho\rho\rho)$, factor $1$; with c.c.; phase $e^{-ik\cdot(y'-w)}$.
Phase $e^{-ik\cdot(y'-w)}$; we first rename $y'\to w'$.
Kernel: $K=\KK(y'-z)\cdot\KK(x-z)\,\KK(x'-y')\cdot\KK(y-w)$.
The symmetrized term (commuting charges):
\begin{align*}
&-2i\NN\int e^{-ik\cdot(y'-w)}\,K\,\Big\{\\
&\qquad (1)\ \ +\,f^{c'd'a}\,U^{e'c'}(y')\,U^{d'b}(z)\,U^{bc}(z)\,U^{ad}(y)\,U^{ce}(x)\,\rho^{e'}(x')\,\rho^{d}(y)\,\rho^{e}(x)\\
&\qquad (2)\ \ -\,f^{c'd'a}\,U^{e'c'}(y')\,U^{d'b}(z)\,U^{ad}(y)\,\rho^{e'}(x')\,\rho^{d}(y)\,\rho^{b}(x)\\
&\qquad (3)\ \ -\,f^{c'd'a}\,U^{bd'}(z)\,U^{c'e'}(x')\,U^{bc}(z)\,U^{ad}(y)\,U^{ce}(x)\,\rho^{e'}(x')\,\rho^{d}(y)\,\rho^{e}(x)\\
&\qquad (4)\ \ +\,f^{c'd'a}\,U^{bd'}(z)\,U^{c'e'}(x')\,U^{ad}(y)\,\rho^{e'}(x')\,\rho^{d}(y)\,\rho^{b}(x)\Big\}+{\rm c.c.}
\end{align*}
\emph{Renaming to common variables} (all replacements simultaneous; $\varepsilon$ is the sign from $\KK(-X)=-\KK(X)$):

mon. & term & \text{renaming} & \text{kernel in common variables} & \varepsilon & \text{class}\\ \hline
 & amp. & y\to x,\ x\to y,\ y'\to w' & \KK(w'-z)\cdot\KK(y-z)\,\KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(x-w) & + & \beta'\\
 & c.c. & x'\to x,\ y\to x',\ x\to y,\ y'\to w,\ w\to w' & \KK(w-z)\cdot\KK(y-z)\,\KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w') & + & \beta\\
\end{tabular}\end{center}

\section{The colour networks, as written}\label{C:sec:networks}

For each class we list every network: its colour structure $C$ (from the first entry in it), the entries in it with their coefficient $c_e$ and sign $s_e$, and the sum $\sum_e c_es_e$ in units of $\NN$. A sum written in red does not vanish. Entries in \textcolor{red}{red} are the ones removed by the corrections of Sec.~\ref{C:sec:corrections}.

\subsection{The non-cancelling networks one by one}

\caption{The 20 networks that do not cancel as written (units of $\NN$).}
\label{C:tab:diag}
\end{table}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{$\alpha_1,\alpha_2$ and $\alpha'_1,\alpha'_2$.} Without the Row II entries these networks cancel, e.g.\ in $\alpha_1$: $\lab{A1.1}\,(-i)+\lab{GII.I1.1b}\,(-i)(-1)=0$. The two Row II entries (monomials b, d of sub-term 3) add $-2i$. They come from the part
\begin{equation}
\mathfrak F^i_\alpha(w;x,y)=-\big[\KK^i(x-w)-\KK^i(y-w)\big]\int_z\KK(x-z)\cdot\KK(y-z)
\label{C:eq:Falpha}
\end{equation}
of the kernel (soft gluon between the two charges) in the term $U^{ah}(w)f^{hbc}\{\rho^b(x),\rho^c(y)\}$, i.e.\ $U(w)\mathbb A^{(3)}$. The same part in the term $\bar{\mathbb A}^{(3)}$ (sub-terms 2, 4) is needed: its entries sit in networks $\alpha_3$, $\alpha_4$, which cancel.
\item \textbf{$\gamma_{1\ldots4}$, $\gamma'_{1\ldots4}$.} Each contains a single entry, from part 2 of Row III. No other term has the kernel $\Phi$, so these entries cannot cancel. In $\gamma_1,\gamma_3$ the colour depends on $z$ through $U(z)$, so the $z$-integral cannot turn them into another class either. Moreover, by Eq.~\eqref{C:eq:Phi}, $\Phi_w(x)=-[(x-w)^2+(x-z)^2]/[(x-w)^2-(x-z)^2]^2$ has a double pole wherever $|x-w|=|x-z|$: in $x$ on the whole perpendicular bisector of $w$ and $z$, in $z$ on the whole circle of radius $|x-w|$ around $x$. Neither the $x$- nor the $z$-integral of these terms exists, so they cannot cancel anything after integration either.
\item \textbf{$\beta_2,\beta_4,\beta_{11},\beta_{12}$ and $\beta'_2,\beta'_4,\beta'_{11},\beta'_{12}$.} Each sums to $\pm2i$. Networks $\beta_{11}$, $\beta_{12}$ contain only Group III entries, which are correct (Sec.~\ref{C:sec:reference}). So a term is missing, with kernel class $\beta$: a soft gluon between the measured gluon at $w$ and a charge. The term $\mathcal D$ of Eq.~\eqref{C:eq:D} supplies exactly $\mp2i$ in each of these networks and nothing elsewhere.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Comparison with an independent leading-log computation}\label{C:sec:reference}
In earlier work on the same cross section we built the $\log(\vee/\Lambda)$ terms row by row directly from the leading-log light-cone wave function, keeping the operator order, and checked those rows numerically against an exact evaluation of the operator products. That construction does not use the intermediate steps of the notes; we call it the \emph{reference}. Its symmetrized three-$\rho$ terms cancel (residual $\sim10^{-14}$), also for non-symmetric $U$. Here we use it only to locate errors: for every row we compare the symmetrized three-$\rho$ content of the notes with that of the reference, kernel monomial by kernel monomial, for random symmetric Wilson lines and charges. A four-$\rho$ term of a row contributes through its three-$\rho$ remainder.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small

Group II, Row I (both terms + its four-$\rho$ part) & $2.4\times10^{-15}$ & 7.0 & agree\\
Group II, Row II (four-$\rho$ part only) & $2.7\times10^{-15}$ & 7.8 & agree\\

Sub-terms 1, 3 of a Group I row are those with the direct amplitude ($U(w)\mathbb A^{(3)}$, $\bar{\mathbb B}_3^\dagger U\mathbb A$, \dots); sub-terms 2, 4 contain the rotated charges and, by unitarity, combine into $\bar{\mathbb A}^{(3)\dagger}$.}
\label{C:tab:reference}

Everything outside Group I agrees with the reference to machine precision. Inside Group I, Row I and the direct parts of Rows IV and V agree as well. The direct parts of Rows II and III and the unitarity combination differ, which matches the three items of Table~\ref{C:tab:diag}.


\section{The two corrections}\label{C:sec:corrections}

\subsection{Correction 1: Row II of Group I}
Split the two-charge kernel of Row II as $\mathfrak F^i=\mathfrak F^i_\beta+\mathfrak F^i_\alpha$, with $\mathfrak F^i_\alpha$ of Eq.~\eqref{C:eq:Falpha} and
\begin{equation}
\mathfrak F^i_\beta(w;x,y)=\KK^i(x-w)\int_z\KK(w-z)\cdot\KK(y-z)-\KK^i(y-w)\int_z\KK(w-z)\cdot\KK(x-z).
\end{equation}
Correction 1: the $U(w)\mathbb A^{(3)}$ term carries $\mathfrak F_\beta$ only,
\begin{align}
\text{Row II}={}&-\frac i2\NN\int e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}\,\KK^i(x'-w')\big[U^{ab'}(x')-U^{ab'}(w')\big]\rho^{b'}(x')\notag\\
&\times\Big[\mathfrak F^i_{\boldsymbol\beta}(w;x,y)\,U^{ah}(w)f^{hbc}\{\rho^b(x),\rho^c(y)\}-\mathfrak F^i(w;x,y)\,f^{abc}U^{bd}(x)U^{ce}(y)\{\rho^d(x),\rho^e(y)\}\Big]+{\rm c.c.}
\label{C:eq:RowIIcorr}
\end{align}
In the tables this deletes the 8 entries \lab{GI.II.1b}\,$(+i)$,\ \lab{GI.II.1d}\,$(-i)$,\ \lab{GI.II.3b}\,$(-i)$,\ \lab{GI.II.3d}\,$(+i)$,\ \lab{GI.II.1b*}\,$(-i)$,\ \lab{GI.II.1d*}\,$(+i)$,\ \lab{GI.II.3b*}\,$(+i)$,\ \lab{GI.II.3d*}\,$(-i)$.

\paragraph{An equivalent form.} One may instead remove $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ from \emph{both} terms of Row II and add the commutator term
\begin{align}
&-i\NN\int e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}\,\KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(s-w)\;\KK(u-z)\cdot\KK(s-z)\notag\\
&\qquad\times f^{eag}\big[U^{ab'}(x')-U^{ab'}(w')\big]\rho^{b'}(x')\,\big\{U^{eh}(u)\rho^h(u),\,U^{gl}(s)\rho^l(s)\big\}+{\rm c.c.},
\label{C:eq:comm}
\end{align}
which is $-[\bar{\mathbb N}_2,\bar{\mathbb A}^{(1)}]$ of the unitarity relation times the conjugate LO amplitude. Its symmetrized three-$\rho$ part equals that of $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ in the $\bar{\mathbb A}^{(3)}$ term. The two forms therefore have the same three-$\rho$ terms (we checked: the corrected Group I agrees with the reference to $1.4\times10^{-14}$ either way), but different two-$\rho$ terms. The three-$\rho$ check cannot decide between them. In the reference this structure arises in the commutator form \eqref{C:eq:comm}, from the operator order of $\bar{\mathbb N}_2$ and $\bar{\mathbb A}^{(1)}$. Part~\ref{part:jimwlk} found that both forms give the same two-$\rho$ terms (Sec.~\ref{T:sec:num}). Sec.~\ref{sec:alpha} shows what correction~1 means: the $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ part is the commutator $[\mathbb N^{(2)},\mathbb A^{(1)}]$, and correction~1 is the $p^+$ ordering of the virtual factor.

\subsection{Correction 2: Row III of Group I}
Delete part 2 of Row III (the 8 entries \lab{GI.III.1b}\,$(+2i)$,\ \lab{GI.III.2b}\,$(-2i)$,\ \lab{GI.III.3b}\,$(-2i)$,\ \lab{GI.III.4b}\,$(+2i)$,\ \lab{GI.III.1b*}\,$(-2i)$,\ \lab{GI.III.2b*}\,$(+2i)$,\ \lab{GI.III.3b*}\,$(+2i)$,\ \lab{GI.III.4b*}\,$(-2i)$) and add
\begin{align}
\mathcal D={}&-2i\NN\int e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}\;\KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(y-w)\;\KK(x-z)\cdot\KK(w-z)\notag\\
&\times f^{abd}\big[U^{ab'}(x')-U^{ab'}(w')\big]\rho^{b'}(x')\;U^{de}(x)\rho^e(x)\;\big[U^{bc}(w)-U^{bc}(y)\big]\rho^c(y)\;+\;{\rm c.c.}
\label{C:eq:D}
\end{align}
$\mathcal D$ has the colour structure of Row III with $U^{bc}(z)\to U^{bc}(w)$. The charge $y$ emits the measured gluon at $w$ at leading order ($\KK(y-w)$, $[U(w)-U(y)]\rho(y)$), and a soft gluon at $z$ is exchanged between the measured gluon and the rotated charge $U(x)\rho(x)$ ($\KK(x-z)\cdot\KK(w-z)$). This is Row III in the region where the gluon emitted by $\mathbb A^{(1)}(p^+,z)$ carries almost all of $k^+$ and becomes the measured gluon, so $z\to w$ in its Wilson line. We believe part 2 of Row III is the notes' version of this region. Its edge kernel $\Phi$ should be replaced by the kernel of Eq.~\eqref{C:eq:D}. In the reference, sub-terms 1, 3 of $\mathcal D$ are a separate virtual row (soft gluon between the measured gluon and $U\rho$), and sub-terms 2, 4 belong to the unitarity combination (Table~\ref{C:tab:after}). For the two-$\rho$ terms of $\mathcal D$, use the charge order of Row III, $\rho(x')\rho(x)\rho(y)$.

\paragraph{Consequence for the two-$\rho$ terms.} Apps.~\ref{R:sec:fourrho}--\ref{R:sec:summary} derive the two-$\rho$ terms from the terms as written. Three groups of them change; all other two-$\rho$ terms stay as they are.
\begin{itemize}
\item The two-$\rho$ terms of Row III, part 2, go away.
\item The two-$\rho$ terms coming from $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ in the $U(w)\mathbb A^{(3)}$ term of Row II go away. With the form \eqref{C:eq:comm}, the $\mathfrak F_\alpha$ terms go away from both terms of Row II, and those of the commutator term are added instead.
\item $\mathcal D$ adds its own two-$\rho$ terms.
\end{itemize}

The entries of $\mathcal D$:
\subsection*{\lab{D}: Replacement $\mathcal D$ of Row III, part 2}\label{C:src:D}
Prefactor $-2i\NN$; ordered product $\rho\rho\rho\to S(\rho\rho\rho)$, factor $1$; with c.c.; phase $e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}$.
Kernel: $K=\KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(y-w)\,\KK(x-z)\cdot\KK(w-z)$.
The symmetrized term (commuting charges):
\begin{align*}
&-2i\NN\int e^{-ik\cdot(w'-w)}\,K\,\Big\{\\
&\qquad (1)\ \ +\,f^{abd}\,U^{ab'}(x')\,U^{de}(x)\,U^{bc}(w)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{e}(x)\,\rho^{c}(y)\\
&\qquad (2)\ \ -\,f^{abd}\,U^{ab'}(x')\,U^{de}(x)\,U^{bc}(y)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{e}(x)\,\rho^{c}(y)\\
&\qquad (3)\ \ -\,f^{abd}\,U^{ab'}(w')\,U^{de}(x)\,U^{bc}(w)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{e}(x)\,\rho^{c}(y)\\
&\qquad (4)\ \ +\,f^{abd}\,U^{ab'}(w')\,U^{de}(x)\,U^{bc}(y)\,\rho^{b'}(x')\,\rho^{e}(x)\,\rho^{c}(y)\Big\}+{\rm c.c.}
\end{align*}
\emph{Renaming to common variables} (all replacements simultaneous; $\varepsilon$ is the sign from $\KK(-X)=-\KK(X)$):
\begin{center}\small\begin{tabular}{@{}l l >{$}l<{$} >{$}l<{$} >{$}c<{$} >{$}c<{$}@{}}
mon. & term & \text{renaming} & \text{kernel in common variables} & \varepsilon & \text{class}\\ \hline
 & amp. & x\to y,\ y\to x & \KK(x'-w')\cdot\KK(x-w)\,\KK(y-z)\cdot\KK(w-z) & + & \beta\\
 & c.c. & x'\to x,\ x\to y,\ y\to x',\ w'\to w,\ w\to w' & \KK(x-w)\cdot\KK(x'-w')\,\KK(y-z)\cdot\KK(w'-z) & + & \beta'\\
\end{tabular}\end{center}
\emph{Entries} (coefficient in units of $\NN$; colour structure in common variables):
\begin{center}\small\begin{longtable}{@{}l >{$}c<{$} >{$}r<{$} >{$}l<{$} l@{}}
entry & \text{class} & \text{coeff.} & \text{colour structure} & \\ \hline\endhead
\lab{D.1} & \beta & -2i & f^{abc}\,U^{ad}(x')\,U^{ce}(y)\,U^{bh}(w)\,\rho^{d}(x')\,\rho^{e}(y)\,\rho^{h}(x) & \\
\lab{D.2} & \beta & +2i & f^{abc}\,U^{ad}(x')\,U^{ce}(y)\,U^{bh}(x)\,\rho^{d}(x')\,\rho^{e}(y)\,\rho^{h}(x) & \\
\lab{D.3} & \beta & +2i & f^{abc}\,U^{ad}(w')\,U^{ce}(y)\,U^{bh}(w)\,\rho^{d}(x')\,\rho^{e}(y)\,\rho^{h}(x) & \\
\lab{D.4} & \beta & -2i & f^{abc}\,U^{ad}(w')\,U^{ce}(y)\,U^{bh}(x)\,\rho^{d}(x')\,\rho^{e}(y)\,\rho^{h}(x) & \\
\lab{D.1*} & \beta' & +2i & f^{abc}\,U^{ad}(x)\,U^{ce}(y)\,U^{bh}(w')\,\rho^{d}(x)\,\rho^{e}(y)\,\rho^{h}(x') & \\

\section{Pointwise numerical check}\label{C:sec:pointwise}

This check does not use the classes or the networks. We pick random points $w,w',z$ and three random charge positions $p_1,p_2,p_3$. We pick random $SU(3)$ adjoint Wilson lines at every point and random classical charges. For every term of the notes, written exactly as in the sources ($\Phi$ as in the notes; for Row II the integrand of the $z$-representation of $\mathfrak F$, taken at the same $z$), we sum over the $3!$ ways of placing its charges on $p_1,p_2,p_3$ and add everything up. The table gives $|\text{sum}|/\sum|\text{terms}|$.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}c c c c c c@{}}
& \multicolumn{3}{c}{symmetric $U$ ($U^{bc}=U^{cb}$)} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{general adjoint $U$}\\
trial & as written & corrected & corrected (form \eqref{C:eq:comm}) & as written & corrected\\ \hline
1 & $1.1\times10^{-1}$ & $3.4\times10^{-16}$ & $3.0\times10^{-16}$ & $4.0\times10^{-2}$ & $3.0\times10^{-3}$\\
2 & $1.9\times10^{-1}$ & $6.1\times10^{-17}$ & $2.7\times10^{-17}$ & $2.0\times10^{-2}$ & $4.7\times10^{-2}$\\
3 & $3.9\times10^{-1}$ & $1.3\times10^{-16}$ & $1.8\times10^{-16}$ & $3.4\times10^{-1}$ & $3.4\times10^{-3}$\\
4 & $5.2\times10^{-2}$ & $8.5\times10^{-17}$ & $8.1\times10^{-17}$ & $9.9\times10^{-2}$ & $1.7\times10^{-2}$\\
